# Evidence

## Baseline and scope

- Branch: `codex/multi-asset-symbol-state`.
- Predecessor/local/remote baseline before work: `f378184761dcb46daa9fce3973c8c837065da150`.
- Clean review clone: `/opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2-trey-rulings-20260830`.
- Operator production checkout `/opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2` was not modified.
- No PM2 operation, broker call/order, service start, live/paper activation, env mutation, reindex, or main-branch operation occurred.

## TDD and focused regression

Initial red run, before implementation:

```text
NODE_PATH=/opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2/node_modules /opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2/node_modules/.bin/jest test/auth-failure-guard.test.js --runInBand --silent
FAIL: guard.wireRuntime is not a function; OrderExecutor had no broker quarantine entry gate.
```

```text
NODE_PATH=/opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2/node_modules /opt/ogzprime/OGZPMLV2/node_modules/.bin/jest test/auth-failure-guard.test.js test/order-executor-pause-gate.test.js --runInBand --silent
PASS test/order-executor-pause-gate.test.js
PASS test/auth-failure-guard.test.js
Test Suites: 2 passed, 2 total
Tests:       135 passed, 135 total

```

Syntax/diff command:

```text
node --check core/AuthFailureGuard.js && node --check core/OrderExecutor.js && node --check run-empire-v2.js && node --check test/auth-failure-guard.test.js && git diff --check
exit 0
```

## Real construction and wired synthetic breach probe

The probe loaded the operator-owned dotenv path without printing values, supplied explicit synthetic Alpaca credentials, constructed `OGZPrimeV14Bot`, and never called `start()`. It verified the guard's real construction-time StateManager and flatten handles before replacing them with an isolated synthetic state and no-order flatten stub. It then injected a three-event threshold breach, retained the healthy Kraken trade, persisted one temporary quarantine flag, reconstructed a second guard against that directory, and drove the real constructed OrderExecutor entry choke. The synthetic sendOrder trap remained at zero calls. The temporary quarantine directory was removed after proof.

Machine log (secured on box, not committed because it contains broad constructor output): `/tmp/part-b-wired-probe.log`

- SHA-256: `77eac588f44112c92ac37b3cd2844b4d05770542050a27e5239ebe23c5deea71`
- Bytes: `24246`

Verdict-bearing output:

```json
{"botConstructed":"OGZPrimeV14Bot","orderExecutorConstructed":"OrderExecutor","guardStateHandleWired":true,"guardFlattenHandleWired":true,"botStarted":false,"syntheticThresholdInjected":true,"flattenCalls":1,"quarantinedBrokerRemoved":true,"healthyBrokerRetained":true,"flagPersisted":true,"secondConstructionBlocked":true,"healthyBrokerBlocked":false,"entryRefused":true,"entryRefusalTrace":true,"alarmTrace":true,"flattenTrace":true,"brokerCallsMade":0,"passed":true}
```

Two earlier probe attempts are named incomplete evidence, not verdicts:

1. The first corrected constructor attempt reached OrderExecutor but stopped before its quarantine gate because SessionRouter's synthetic scope lacked `accountId`.
2. The second reached entry policy but stopped at the pre-existing `StateManager.isTrading=false` constructor-safe posture.

Both were diagnosed from their exact non-secret errors; the final probe supplied a complete synthetic scope and temporarily returned `isTrading=true` only to reach the quarantine choke. Neither incomplete attempt called a broker.

## Real max-priority ntfy delivery

A one-time HTTPS POST used only the existing `NTFY_TOPIC` loaded from the operator-owned dotenv path. Neither endpoint nor topic was printed or committed. Message: a synthetic Part B AuthFailureGuard quarantine proof explicitly stating no broker call/order occurred. Request priority was `max`.

Machine receipt: `/tmp/part-b-ntfy-receipt.json`

- Receipt SHA-256: `18d8593c5a1ca407b697e3eee06d78bc8593ae030e664069b0afd7070bafbcba`
- Receipt bytes: `340`
- HTTP status: `200`
- Response ID: `1WflymsbMj4U`
- Response event: `message`
- Original transport response body SHA-256: `2b79c23b70ce5c7a815dd0de8e0e6e108572c89fafd172f272a986fba5b1f16f`
- Original response bytes: `307`
- Endpoint present: true; endpoint disclosed: false.

## Named absences and limitations

- No real credential failure was induced and no broker position was flattened; doing so would violate the no-broker-call/no-order boundary. Synthetic flatten plus existing-path wiring were proven separately.
- Broker positions absent from StateManager's broker-owned `activeTrades` cannot be safely flattened through the existing state-accounted execute path. No second/orphan flatten capability was invented. Such an external-state mismatch remains operator reconciliation territory.
- No PM2/restart proof was performed. Restart persistence was proven by on-disk flag creation and a second `AuthFailureGuard` construction/read.
- The notifier's existing async trace subscriber does not return its HTTP response. The authorized live delivery therefore used the same endpoint resolution and Node HTTPS transport semantics in a receipt-bearing one-time probe.

## Adversarial evidence

The full live layer reviewed the nine-file intent-to-add diff first, then one focused replacement run resolved the sole remaining dispute.

1. Broad run `2026-08-30T16-03-08-075Z-a5115da3ee04`
   - Raw directory: `ogz-meta/cognition-history/mercury-runs/raw/2026-08-30/2026-08-30t16-01-37-164z-1465258-d3cf47c54c53/`
   - Ledger: `ogz-meta/cognition-history/mercury-runs/2026-08-30.jsonl:7`
   - Mercury termination `answer_given`, 17 iterations, no provider/tool failure.
   - Genuine Fable applied model `claude-fable-5`; auxiliary telemetry `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`; Fable verdict seat `claude-fable-5`; identity matched/full authority; no tools in Fable stage.
   - Kimi applied model `kimi-k3` because Fable and Mercury remained unresolved.
   - Effective panel verdict: `needs_more_evidence`. Required resolution: determine whether missing-config import failure was authored Part B behavior or pre-existing intentional fail-fast behavior.
2. Focused reconciliation run `2026-08-30T16-04-59-906Z-8ff1c235f45b`
   - Raw directory: `ogz-meta/cognition-history/mercury-runs/raw/2026-08-30/2026-08-30t16-03-42-951z-1465673-5932e5cc1af9/`
   - Ledger: `ogz-meta/cognition-history/mercury-runs/2026-08-30.jsonl:8`
   - Mercury termination `answer_given`, 8 iterations, no provider/tool failure.
   - Genuine Fable applied model `claude-fable-5`; auxiliary telemetry `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`; Fable verdict seat `claude-fable-5`; identity matched/full authority; no tools in Fable stage.
   - Kimi applied model `kimi-k3` to adjudicate Fable's baseline correction.
   - Final effective verdict: `pass`, blocking false, no required rechecks.

Every ledger line and raw provider file for both runs is embedded below after `redactSensitiveText`. The manifest records original-on-box and committed-redacted SHA-256/bytes for every payload. Secured originals remain mode 0600 in the raw directories. No raw provider file was silently omitted.

Mechanical packet verification reproduced 57 manifest rows and 57 payload blocks, independently rehashed every committed payload with zero mismatches, and confirmed all 57 committed payloads are invariant under a second `redactSensitiveText` pass. The generated tape section before insertion was 693711 bytes with SHA-256 `e8519d81a82afb4c21173ef6b233f6bee7feba3b3746e5e668271d7df9ba88de`.

`npm run scan:secrets` found no Part B packet path after the additional `apiKeySource` metadata redaction. The repository-wide command remains nonzero on 15 pre-existing findings: seven `apiKeySource` metadata false positives in Part A's already-committed packet and eight existing Mercury test fixtures. Those paths are outside Part B territory and are named inherited failures rather than silently presented as a passing repository-wide scan.

Do not edit code.\nMercury, recheck your prior answer against Fable critique.\n\nOriginal user prompt:\nMercury, break my fix.\n\nHost-attested evidence manifest (the verbatim excerpts are supplied inside the original prompt above; they were not opened by model tools):\nnone\n\nYour prior answer:\n**Concrete break**\n\nIf the `authFailureGuard` block is omitted from the runtime configuration, the module `core/AuthFailureGuard.js` throws an exception during import, which brings down every component that `require`s it (e.g., `brokers/AlpacaAdapter.js`, `kraken_adapter_simple.js`, `run‑empire‑v2.js`, `core/OrderExecutor.js`).  \n\n*Evidence*  \n\n1. **`core/AuthFailureGuard.js` validates the config on load** – `loadConfig()` reads `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` and throws when the block is missing or malformed:\n\n   ```js\n   function loadConfig() {\n     const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n     if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     ...[truncated 4886 chars]","recheck_prompt_full":"READ-ONLY AUDIT. Do not edit code.\nMercury, recheck your prior answer against Fable critique.\n\nOriginal user prompt:\nMercury, break my fix.\n\nHost-attested evidence manifest (the verbatim excerpts are supplied inside the original prompt above; they were not opened by model tools):\nnone\n\nYour prior answer:\n**Concrete break**\n\nIf the `authFailureGuard` block is omitted from the runtime configuration, the module `core/AuthFailureGuard.js` throws an exception during import, which brings down every component that `require`s it (e.g., `brokers/AlpacaAdapter.js`, `kraken_adapter_simple.js`, `run‑empire‑v2.js`, `core/OrderExecutor.js`).  \n\n*Evidence*  \n\n1. **`core/AuthFailureGuard.js` validates the config on load** – `loadConfig()` reads `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` and throws when the block is missing or malformed:\n\n   ```js\n   function loadConfig() {\n     const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n     if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n       throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n     }\n     // …\n   }\n   ```\n   (lines 46‑50 of `core/AuthFailureGuard.js`core/AuthFailureGuard.js:46-50)\n\n2. **`core/AuthFailureGuard.js` is required by many production files** – the blast‑radius report shows 15 callers, e.g.:\n\n   - `brokers/AlpacaAdapter.js` (line 21)  \n   - `kraken_adapter_simple.js` (line 41)  \n   - `run-empire-v2.js` (line 262)  \n   - `core/OrderExecutor.js` (line 15)  \n\n   (see the “Blast Radius” summary in the prompt)\n\n3. **If the config block is absent, the `require` call fails** – any of the above files will throw at import time, preventing the process from starting. This is a deterministic failure that can be reproduced by removing the `authFailureGuard` entry from `config/trading.config.json` and running the application.\n\nTherefore, the current implementation **breaks** when the `authFailureGuard` configuration is missing, which is a realistic scenario (e.g., a new deployment that forgets to copy the block).   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; cache...[truncated 1138 chars]","answer_full":"**CONCRETE_BREAK_FOUND**\n\n*Why the import‑time crash occurs*\n\n1. **`AuthFailureGuard` loads its configuration in the constructor**  \n   ```js\n   constructor({ quarantineDir = DEFAULT_QUARANTINE_DIR } = {}) {\n     this.config = loadConfig();          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import. `brokers/AlpacaAdapter.js` (line 21)  \n   - `kraken_adapter_simple.js` (line 41)  \n   - `run-empire-v2.js` (line 262)  \n   - `core/OrderExecutor.js` (line 15)  \n\n   (see the “Blast Radius” summary in the prompt)\n\n3. **If the config block is absent, the `require` call fails** – any of the above files will throw at import time, preventing the process from starting. This is a deterministic failure that can be reproduced by removing the `authFailureGuard` entry from `config/trading.config.json` and running the application.\n\nTherefore, the current implementation **breaks** when the `authFailureGuard` configuration is missing, which is a realistic scenario (e.g., a new deployment that forgets to copy the block).   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import.   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cache...[truncated 1138 chars]","answer_full":"**CONCRETE_BREAK_FOUND**\n\n*Why the import‑time crash occurs*\n\n1. **`AuthFailureGuard` loads its configuration in the constructor**  \n   ```js\n   constructor({ quarantineDir = DEFAULT_QUARANTINE_DIR } = {}) {\n     this.config = loadConfig();          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. cache...[truncated 1138 chars]","answer_full":"**CONCRETE_BREAK_FOUND**\n\n*Why the import‑time crash occurs*\n\n1. **`AuthFailureGuard` loads its configuration in the constructor**  \n   ```js\n   constructor({ quarantineDir = DEFAULT_QUARANTINE_DIR } = {}) {\n     this.config = loadConfig();          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import. `brokers/AlpacaAdapter.js` (line 21)  \n   - `kraken_adapter_simple.js` (line 41)  \n   - `run-empire-v2.js` (line 262)  \n   - `core/OrderExecutor.js` (line 15)  \n\n   (see the “Blast Radius” summary in the prompt)\n\n3. **If the config block is absent, the `require` call fails** – any of the above files will throw at import time, preventing the process from starting.          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import. This deterministic failure matches the original claim, so the **break** is validated."},{"path":"mercury://recheck-1-telemetry","sha256":"8e0d6625d63d1d277140bf527d77f813567b0fecafc744264c5c9ad416f727b1","bytes":156,"excerpt":"tool_calls=13 | succeeded=13 | failed=0 | tools=open_file:7/7/0, search:6/6/0 | files_opened=7 | run_check_artifacts=0 | failed_calls=none | run_checks=none"}]},"raw_output":{"path":"ogz-meta/cognition-history/mercury-runs/raw/2026-08-30/2026-08-30t16-01-37-164z-1465258-d3cf47c54c53/kimi_tie_breaker-1.raw","sha256":"3df4bb538582ba3422370e4752a007697b85b81f5920ebe374a67de4fe87b5b4","bytes":2165,"mode":"0600"},"raw_error":null,"tools":{"enabled":false,"available":[],"calls":[],"total":0,"succeeded":0,"failed":0},"files_mechanically_opened":[],"claimed_file_citations":[],"auth_posture":null,"executable_trust":null,"error":null,"repo_adjudication":{"status":"pending","authority":"live_repo_required"}},"quarantines":[],"repo_adjudication":{"status":"pending","authority":"live_repo_required"},"parsed":{"consensus":"none","contradictions":"- Fable challenges Mercury's claim that `AuthFailureGuard.js` throws during import, arguing the cited lines 46-50 only show a function definition without proof of module-scope execution. This is a deterministic failure that can be reproduced by removing the `authFailureGuard` entry from `config/trading.config.json` and running the application.\n\nTherefore, the current implementation **breaks** when the `authFailureGuard` configuration is missing, which is a realistic scenario (e.g., a new deployment that forgets to copy the block). The fix that only validates the block but does not provide a safe fallback is insufficient.\n\nFable critique:\nVERDICT: needs_more_evidence\nCONSENSUS_BLOCKING: yes\nRATIONALE: Mercury's \"break\" hinges entirely on the claim that `AuthFailureGuard.js` \"throws during import,\" but the only cited evidence (lines 46-50) is a **function definition** of `loadConfig()` — Mercury never shows a call site proving `loadConfig()` executes at module scope rather than lazily (constructor/first-use), which is the difference between a process-killing import break and intended fail-fast validation. cache...[truncated 1138 chars]","answer_full":"**CONCRETE_BREAK_FOUND**\n\n*Why the import‑time crash occurs*\n\n1. **`AuthFailureGuard` loads its configuration in the constructor**  \n   ```js\n   constructor({ quarantineDir = DEFAULT_QUARANTINE_DIR } = {}) {\n     this.config = loadConfig();          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import. {"id":"[REDACTED]","object":"chat.completion","created":1788105773,"model":"mercury-2","choices":[{"index":0,"message":{"role":"assistant","content":"**CONCRETE_BREAK_FOUND**\n\n*Why the import‑time crash occurs*\n\n1. **`AuthFailureGuard` loads its configuration in the constructor**  \n   ```js\n   constructor({ quarantineDir = DEFAULT_QUARANTINE_DIR } = {}) {\n     this.config = loadConfig();          // ← called here\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L66-L73】\n\n2. **An instance is created at module top‑level**, so the constructor (and thus `loadConfig`) runs **as soon as the file is `require`‑d**.  \n   ```js\n   const authFailureGuard = new AuthFailureGuard();   // ← module‑scope execution\n   module.exports = authFailureGuard;\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L278-L280】\n\n3. **`loadConfig` reads the block via `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')`**.  \n   ```js\n   const block = ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard');\n   if (!block || typeof block !== 'object' || Array.isArray(block)) {\n     throw new Error('AuthFailureGuard: missing authFailureGuard block in ConfigLoader');\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L46-L50】\n\n4. **`ConfigLoader.get` simply walks the cached configuration object**; it does **not** supply a default value.  \n   ```js\n   function get(path) {\n     if (!_cached) load();\n     const parts = path.split('.');\n     let val = _cached.config;\n     for (const part of parts) {\n       if (val === undefined || val === null) return undefined;\n       val = val[part];\n     }\n     return val;\n   }\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L1550-L1558】\n\n5. **The repository’s configuration file contains the required block**, confirming that a missing block would make `ConfigLoader.get('authFailureGuard')` return `undefined` and trigger the error above.  \n   ```json\n   \"authFailureGuard\": {\n     \"thresholdCount\": 3,\n     \"windowMs\": 300000\n   },\n   ```  \n   【open_file†L16-L19】\n\n*Conclusion*  \nBecause `AuthFailureGuard.js` creates an `AuthFailureGuard` instance at the top level, the constructor (and `loadConfig`) executes during the `require` call. If the `authFailureGuard` entry is absent from `config/trading.config.json`, `ConfigLoader.get` returns `undefined`, causing `loadConfig` to throw and abort the import. This deterministic failure matches the original claim, so the **break** is validated.","tool_calls":null,"tool_call_id":null},"finish_reason":"stop"}],"usage":{"prompt_tokens":19519,"reasoning_tokens":341,"completion_tokens":544,"total_tokens":20404,"cached_input_tokens":6},"warning":null,"reasoning_summary":null}
